廣達(2382 TT) Trading Buy

AI 伺服器展望維持不變，投資建議 Trading Buy 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，
且考慮到 CSP 對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料
能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin 將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，2027
年營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於資料中心建置
的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy 的
投資建議，目標價 324 元。 
3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，稅後 EPS 6.92 元：3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，
QoQ+7.6%；毛利率 4.75%， QoQ-0.27ppts；營業利益 322.04 億
元，QoQ-6.0%；歸屬母公司淨利 284.22 億元，QoQ-0.8%；稅後
EPS 6.92 元。 
PC 市場需求疲弱，伺服器業務支撐營收季增： 3Q26 筆電出貨
960 萬台，QoQ-17%，考慮到筆電零組件漲價帶動 ASP 提高，
我們預估 PC 營收季減高個位數。通用型伺服器營收優於預期，
除 2H26 AI Agent 強勁的需求外，ASP 亦由於零組件漲價而提
升。AI 伺服器部分出貨量較 2Q26 微幅下滑，但同樣受零組件
漲價帶動 ASP 提升影響，預估 AI 伺服器營收仍季增低個位數。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：近期市場對於限制資料中心相關法案
的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單，就現
階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI
伺服器展望將維持不變。我們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin 將可如先
前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收高雙位數增長。考慮
到 AI Agent 的需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器全年
營收呈雙位數年增。 
預估 2027 年營收 5.99 兆元，稅後 EPS 30.85 元：群益預估 2027

2 群益投顧 
  ESG 
廣達積極推動環境、社會及公司治理（ESG）永續發展，並將節能減碳、人
才培育及供應鏈管理納入企業營運策略。環境方面，公司於 2024 年通過科
學基礎減碳目標倡議（SBTi）審查，承諾以 2022 年為基準，2030 年前將範
疇一及範疇二溫室氣體排放量降低 42%、範疇三降低 25%，並以 2050 年達
成淨零排放為長期目標。公司亦持續推動再生能源使用及能源管理，2024 年
綠電使用占總用電量 47.51%，並設定 2030 年再生能源使用比例達 50%的目
標。 
社會責任方面，廣達重視員工權益、職業安全及人才培育，遵循責任商業聯
盟（ RBA）行為準則，並透過廣達文教基金會及廣藝基金會推動教育與藝文
活動。公司治理方面，廣達持續強化風險管理、資訊安全及供應鏈永續管理，
並參照 TCFD 及 SASB 等國際框架揭露永續資訊，以提升營運透明度及長期
經營韌性。 
公司簡介 
廣達電腦（Quanta Computer Inc.， 2382 TT）成立於 1988 年，總部位於台灣
桃園，為全球主要電子產品原廠設計製造（ODM）廠商之一，主要業務涵蓋
筆記型電腦、伺服器、雲端運算設備及其他智慧電子產品。公司早期以筆記
型電腦代工為主要業務，憑藉產品研發、供應鏈管理及全球製造能力，建立
與國際品牌客戶的長期合作關係，近年則積極擴展雲端運算及人工智慧相
關業務。 
廣達透過旗下雲達科技（QCT）布局資料中心市場，提供伺服器、儲存設備、
網路交換器及整機櫃解決方案，並隨大型雲端服務供應商（CSP）擴 大 AI 基
礎建設投資，逐步深化 AI 伺服器業務布局。相較傳統筆電代工，AI 伺服器
對系統整合、散熱、電源管理及高速互連技術要求較高，帶動產品複雜度及
單機價值提升，成為公司營收成長的重要驅動因素。 
展望未來，廣達持續推進 AI 伺服器及機櫃級系統整合能力，並透過全球生
產據點布局，強化供應鏈韌性及在地化交付能力。隨 AI 運算需求持續擴張，
伺服器業務可望維持公司中長期成長動能，惟仍須關注 CSP 資本支出、產
品轉換時程及新平台量產效率對營運的影響。 
3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.08 元：3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，QoQ+7.6%，
YoY+125.2%；毛利率 4.75%，QoQ-0.27ppts，YoY-2.10ppts；營業利益 322.04
億元，QoQ-6.0%，YoY+75.3%，營益率 2.89%，QoQ-0.41ppts，YoY-0.82ppts；
歸屬母公司淨利 284.22 億元，QoQ-0.8%， YoY+73.0%；稅後 EPS 7.08 元。
PC 市場需求疲弱，伺服器業務支撐營收季增： 3Q26 筆電出貨 960 萬台，
QoQ-17%， YoY-24%，出貨量略優於 2Q26 預期的 925 萬台，考慮到筆電零
組件漲價帶動 ASP 提高，我們預估 PC 營收季減高個位數。通用型伺服器營
收優於預期，主要原因除 2H26 AI Agent 強勁的需求外，亦由於 ASP 受零
組件漲價影響而提升。AI 伺服器部分出貨量較 2Q26 微幅下滑，但同樣受零
組件漲價帶動 ASP 提升影響，預估 AI 伺服器營收仍季增低個位數。 
零組件漲價及產品組合改變導致毛利率承壓：由於(1)筆電持續因成本上揚
而壓縮獲利，且出貨數下滑不利經濟規模(2)雖 AI 伺服器出貨量季減，但由

3 群益投顧 
ASP 提升驅動營收增長，造成毛利率稀釋(3)通用型伺服器出貨量小幅季增，
且 ASP 提升。綜上原因導致 3Q26 毛利率季減 0.27ppts，帶動營益率季減
0.41ppts，此一情景預估將延續至 2027 年，且我們預估 Vera Rubin 世代平
台 ASP 較 GB300 系列接近倍增，雖營收與獲利將可持續上升，但未來 margin
將持續承壓。雖目前有數個專案採用 Consignment model，然而主要以通用
型伺服器與 ASIC 平台為主，且出貨形式由 L6 至 L11 皆有，營收貢獻相對
於採用 B/S 的整櫃 GPU 伺服器而言佔比極低。據我們了解目前已有確定一
個 Vera Rubin 的大型專案及數個規模較小的專案將採用 Consignment 
model，但預估占整體伺服器營收比重仍低，對於毛利率的改善效果有限。
由於 B/S 將稀釋毛利率，Consignment 則會使營收下滑，因此未來針對 ODM
的獲利能力的評估重點應轉向以營業利益年複合成長率(CAGR)或是 ROIC
為主。 
終止華亞科技園區收購，2028 年產能擴充計畫維持不變：廣達表示，由於
對廠方規格及交付時程等事項無法取得共識，因此撤銷友達華亞科技園區
廠房的收購案，原先規劃的產能將透過海外投資計畫或其他彈性應變方案，
維持 2028 年投產的計畫不變。廣達 3Q26 資本支出 128 億元，2026 前三季
累計資本支出達 346 億元，預估 2027 年資本支出介於 400-450 億元，投資
金額成長幅度收斂，將主要用於改良租賃廠房及電力升級。管理層表示 AI
擴廠計畫為配合 CSP 客戶未來 12-24 個月的需求規劃，且伺服器屬於客製
化接單生產(Build-to-Order)，非承擔庫存風險的標準品倍或生產，資本支出
主要投入的項目為廠房電力設施及高階冷卻系統等通用的基礎設施，因此
縱然單一客戶或是特定世代的 AI 伺服器平台需求放緩，亦可無縫轉換產能，
較不會出現過去供過於求時，閒置產能導致的折舊與維護費用等沉沒成本，
營運風險相對較小。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：美國期中選舉後，市場對於限制資料中心相關法
案的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單。廣達的客戶以五
大 CSP 為主，Neocloud 的占比不高，就現階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚
未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI 伺服器展望將維持不變。3Q26 AI 伺服器出貨
量的下滑主要原因為新舊平台交替的陣痛期，預估 4Q26 伺服器出貨量仍將
下滑，但因 Vera Rubin 相較 GB300 近乎倍增的 ASP，AI 伺服器營收將維持
季增。此外，目前在記憶體、PCB 及 CPU 缺貨問題對於消費性產品及通用
型伺服器影響較大，但由於廣達至 2027 年的訂單人以五大 CSP 為主，且
CSP 巨頭鎖料能力較強，因此 AI 伺服器的長短料問題相較之下較輕微，我
們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin將可如先前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收
高雙位數增長。通用型伺服器部分，受惠 AI Agent 的應用落地帶動通用型
伺服器需求增長，預估 4Q26 通用型伺服器將持續季增。考慮到 AI Agent 的
需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器除 Share gain 外，亦受惠整體市
場需求提升帶來的成長，全年營收呈雙位數年增。 
管理層預期 2027 年筆電市場持續低迷：管理層預期 2027 年筆電需求將持
續受料件漲價及缺料等問題壓抑，導致 2027 年筆電出貨量下滑高個位數至
低雙位數左右，考慮到目前筆電營收占比已不足 15%，因此我們認為 2027
年合併營收將受伺服器的強勁成長帶動而年增。 
預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.19 元：預期 4Q26 筆電市場仍舊低
迷，AI 伺服器處於世代交替的轉換期，出貨量將持續下滑，但受 ASP 提升
影響而帶動 AI 伺服器營收季增，此外通用型伺服器出貨亦維持強勁。綜上

4 群益投顧 
所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，QoQ+7.0%：毛利率 4.62%， QoQ-
0.13ppts，毛利續持續下滑主要受到 AI 伺服器 ASP 提升帶來的稀釋，以及
筆電出貨量下滑造成的規模經濟效果衰減所影響；營業利益 331.68 億元，
QoQ+3.0%，營益率 2.78%；歸屬母公司淨利 288.35 億元，QoQ+1.45%；稅
後 EPS 7.02 元。 
預估 2027 年營收 5.99 兆元，稅後 EPS 30.85 元：群益預估 2027 年合併營
收 5.99 兆元，YoY+44.1%；毛利率 4.18%，YoY-0.6ppts；營業利益 1,490.08
億元，YoY+21.5%，營益率 2.49%， YoY-0.5ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 1,266.99
億元，YoY+18.3%，稅後 EPS 30.85 元。 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，且考慮到 CSP
對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin
將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收維持高雙位數成
長。通用型伺服器則受惠於 AI Agent 帶動運算需求及市占提升，預估全年
營收呈雙位數年增。預期伺服器強勁的成長將可抵銷筆電市場的低迷，帶動
2027 年營收年增，惟 Vera Rubin 的高單價將使毛利率及營益率承壓，但營
業利益亦將仍持續增長，整體營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於
資料中心建置的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy
的投資建議，目標價 324。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on preliminary JMTBA orders, which we
believe could be a catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
According to the Japan Machine Tool Builders' Association (JMTBA), headline

orders dropped 8% MoM but grew 43% YoY in October  2026 (vs. +13% MoM/
+60% YoY in September 2026), to ¥204bn.
• Domestic orders were -18% MoM/+47% YoY (vs. +24% MoM/+46% YoY in
September), at ¥52bn.
• Overseas orders were -5% MoM/+41% YoY (vs. +9% MoM/+67% YoY  in
September), at ¥152bn.
Our view:
• For overseas orders, the growth remains solid on a YoY basis. We think the
sequential decline is likely from China as its manufacturing PMI edged down

to 49.3 in Oct (vs. 50.1 in Sep). Meanwhile, North America and Europe could
prove more resilient, with manufacturing PMIs staying relatively firm at
55.5/52.7 in Oct (vs. 54.5/52.9 in Sep)
• We remain OW on both Airtac (1590.TW) and Hiwin (2049.TW), as we see
company-specific drivers on top of the industry recovery trend:
° Airtac should continue to benefit from its own market share gains.
Valuation also appears undemanding at 15x 2027e P/E (vs. 25x average

since 2020).
° Hiwin should  see margin expansion in the coming quarters, driven by
higher utilization and price hikes. Current valuation of 27x 2027 P/E
implies upside vs. peak cycle valuation at 35-40x P/E.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 25x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). We think our valuation of 25x P/E is reasonable (vs. average of 25x since
2020).
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China
n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation

Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple
of 34x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 50% operating profit

CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup
n Market share gains
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand
n More pricing pressure
n Market share loss
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks

the next 12-18 months.

universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

12-18 months.

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

優群 3Q26 獲利低於預期，主要受筆電需求疲弱、稼動率下降及原
物料成本上漲影響，預期 4Q26 營運仍將承壓。惟隨 Google 伺服
器新案及網通新產品於 2027 年放量，加上高單價新產品陸續導
入，營收與獲利可望重返成長。研究部下修優群 2026、2027 年稅
後 EPS 至 10.06 元（原估 11.31 元）及 11.83 元（原估 14.30 
元）。考量短期利空已逐步反映於股價，現價對應 2027 年本益比
僅 8.8 倍，低於歷史區間下緣，且預估殖利率約 7.6%，具備下檔
支撐及評價修復空間。研究部以 2027 年 EPS 為評價基礎，給予 
12 倍本益比，目標價 140 元，投資評等由中立調升至買進。

重點摘要： 
1. 筆電需求疲弱、稼動率下降，3Q26 獲利低於預期 
優群 3Q26 營收 9.75 億元（+2.9% QoQ、−13.8% YoY），低於原
估 10.90 億元，主因筆電客戶持續調整出貨，SO-DIMM 與 M.2 營
收分別年減 36% 及 20%，抵銷伺服器與新產品成長貢獻。受營收
不如預期、產能利用率下降及原物料成本上漲影響，毛利率降至 
46.1%（−1.5ppt QoQ、−5.1ppt YoY），低於原估 47.4%；營益率
降至 27.8%，低於原估 29.9%。稅後淨利 2.55 億元（+18.9% QoQ、
−32.8% YoY），稅後 EPS 2.84 元，低於原估 3.42 元。

表一、優群 3Q26 獲利檢討及 4Q26 獲利修正

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 筆電需求持續疲弱，下修 4Q26 獲利預估 
優群 10 月營收 2.58 億元（−22.9% MoM、−25.9% YoY），主要受
筆電客戶持續調整出貨影響。預期 11、12 月出貨將較 10 月回
升，惟整體需求仍偏弱，產能利用率下降將持續壓抑毛利率表現。
考量筆電需求修正幅度超出預期，研究部下修 4Q26 營收預估至 
8.80 億元（−9.8% QoQ、−15.5% YoY，原估 10.26 億元），毛利
率由原估 47.8% 下修至 45.4%，稅後 EPS 由 2.69 元下修至 
2.02 元（−29.3% QoQ、−37.9% YoY）。

3. CSP 伺服器與網通新產品放量，2027 年營運結構持續優化 
隨 AI 推論及企業運算需求成長，伺服器對記憶體容量、頻寬及傳
輸效能的要求持續提升，帶動 DDR5 R-DIMM 朝高速化與高容量發
展。相較傳統 U-DIMM，R-DIMM 對訊號完整性及可靠度要求較高，
連接器製程與品質門檻亦隨之提升，有利高單價產品需求成長。優
群持續拓展 Meta 與 Google 等 CSP 客戶訂單，2026 年 R-DIMM 
累計出貨量已超越 2025 年全年，且高單價 R-DIMM 占比持續提
升，有助優化產品組合。其中，Google 伺服器新案預計於 1Q27 在
越南廠量產，除帶動 Long-DIMM 出貨成長，亦有助提升越南廠稼
動率並改善規模經濟，研究部預估 2027 年 Long-DIMM 營收成長 
30.9% YoY。網通方面，優群持續拓展既有筆電與伺服器以外的通
訊設備應用，新產品已於 3Q26 開始量產，預期 2027 年隨客戶訂
單增加及新應用拓展，出貨規模持續擴大。搭配 CAMM2、CCBB 等
新產品貢獻，研究部預估 2027 年 Others 營收成長 33.8% YoY，
成為營運結構轉型的重要成長來源。相較之下，傳統筆電 SO-DIMM 
營收預估衰退 2.4% YoY，顯示營運成長動能逐步由筆電轉向伺服
器與網通應用，有助降低對筆電景氣的依賴。

圖一、優群產品營收比重（2024～2027F）

資料來源：優群，統一投顧預估

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 記憶體模組化與可維修設計趨勢，帶動高單價產品滲透 
隨筆電朝輕薄化、高速傳輸及可維修設計發展，連接器規格持續升
級，帶動高單價產品導入。記憶體方面，傳統 SO-DIMM 受限於模
組高度與訊號傳輸需求，部分高階筆電轉向焊接式 LPDDR 設計，
壓抑傳統連接器需求；CAMM2 則兼具低矮化、高速傳輸及模組可更
換等優勢，提供新一代記憶體連接方案。優群 CAMM2 目前月出貨
約 5～10 萬顆，HP 約 10 款高階機種正進行認證，預計 1H27 量
產，隨模組供應改善及新機種導入，可望擴大出貨規模。Type-C 方
面，隨 USB-C 成為筆電充電與資料傳輸的主要介面，接口維修成
本逐漸受到品牌重視。優群推出可獨立拆卸的兩件式 Type-C，無
須因接口損壞而更換整片主機板，單價約為傳統產品的兩倍，目前
已導入聯想機種，HP 預計於 2027 年加入。此外，Metal Bar 微
型沖壓件持續由筆電拓展至記憶體及 SiP 模組，憑藉精密沖壓與
微型化製程能力，擴大高毛利產品應用。研究部預估 2027 年 
Metal Bar 營收成長約 18% YoY，隨高單價新產品滲透率提升，有
助優化產品組合並支撐毛利率回升。

5. 筆電需求修正超出預期，下修 2026 年獲利，2027 年重返成
長 
考量筆電需求疲弱、客戶持續調整出貨，加上稼動率下降及原物料
成本上漲壓抑獲利，研究部下修優群 2026 年營收預估至 37.50 
億元（−9.6% YoY，原估 40.11 億元），其中 SO-DIMM 與 M.2 營
收分別衰退 29% 及 17% YoY，為主要拖累；毛利率由原估 47.9% 
下修至 47.0%，稅後淨利預估 9.03 億元（−21.0% YoY），稅後 
EPS 由 11.31 元下修至 10.06 元。 
展望 2027 年，隨 CSP 伺服器新案放量、網通業務擴張及高單價
新產品持續貢獻，研究部預估 Long-DIMM、Metal Bar 及其他產品
營收分別成長約 31%、18% 及 34% YoY，帶動全年營收回升至 
41.12 億元（+9.7% YoY，原估 46.67 億元）。隨營收規模擴大、
稼動率提升及產品組合改善，毛利率預估回升至 47.6%，稅後淨利 
10.54 億元（+16.8% YoY），稅後 EPS 11.83 元（+16.8% YoY，
原估 14.30 元）。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 短期利空已逐步反映，低評價與高殖利率浮現投資價值 
考量筆電需求疲弱及短期獲利下修，優群股價已大幅修正，現價對
應 2027 年本益比僅 8.8 倍，低於歷史 10～20 倍區間下緣，反
映短期營運利空已逐步消化。此外，優群近五年現金配息率約 78%
～79%，以研究部預估 2026 年 EPS 10.06 元推算，現金股利約 
7.9 元，按 11/18 收盤價 104.5 元計算，預估殖利率約 7.6%，
提供下檔支撐。展望 2027 年，隨 CSP 伺服器與網通新業務放量，
營收及獲利可望重返成長，帶動評價修復。綜合考量短期利空已逐
步反映、獲利成長動能回升及殖利率支撐，研究部以 2027 年 EPS 
為評價基礎，給予 12 倍本益比，目標價 140 元，投資評等由中
立調升至買進。

虹堡 (5258 TT) 
2027 年營運重回成長

永豐觀點 
目前訂單能見度約一季，較先前有所改善。美洲客戶需求持續看增，預估明年成長
10%-20%。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進投資建議：主係考量(1) 新客戶訂單將逐漸貢獻營收，並持續精簡費用，
提升管理效率，2027 年獲利成長可期。(2) 主要營收區域歐洲及北美地區訂單狀
況皆轉好。評價上，以 2027 年稅後 EPS 5.04 元計算，目前本益比約 8 倍，明顯
低於過去五年本益比區間 10-17 倍，考量營運將重返成長，故給予 10 倍本益比
(原 11 倍)，維持買進的投資建議，下修目標價至 50 元。 
 近八季營收及 YoY 趨勢圖

### Page 2

2

全球前 15 大 POS 終端機製造業者：虹堡成立於 1993 年總部位於新北市新店區，
主要從事 POS 設備及相關產品之設計、開發與銷售，為台灣電子金融交易終端機
(POS)及 IC 晶片卡讀卡機製造商。主要營業項目為電子金融交易終端機(POS)及相
關產品之設計、開 發、銷售，這類終端機通過國際組織認證後經客戶在全球各地
通過當地收單機構驗證後即可用做當地商家收單使用。產品目前可支援各類型的支
付方式，包含非接觸卡片(contactless card)、晶片卡(chip card)、磁條卡(magnetic 
strip card)及新興的二維條碼及指紋辨識支付等。虹堡產品可概分為電子金融交易
終端機、個人金融應用產品、電子式收銀機及週邊設備等。國內主要競爭對手：振
樺電、樺漢、飛捷、伍豐等。 
記憶體價格飆漲，3Q26 獲利低於預期：在歐洲地區需求略有回穩下，虹堡 3Q26
營收 24.25 億元(+3.3%QoQ，+18.4%YoY)，受到記憶體價格上漲影響，毛利率
31.5%(+3pts QoQ，+4.6pts YoY)，因費用控管得宜，營利率 9.9%(+3.1pts QoQ，
+3.1pts YoY)，稅後淨利 1.5 億元(+25%QoQ，+8.1%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 1.36 元，
低於預期的 1.52 元。 
物料狀況預期轉好，4Q26 持續成長：虹堡 10 月營收 6.83 億元(-27.4%MoM，-
1.8%YoY)，衰退係受原物料價格上漲及法國動亂影響，持累積第四季達成率
27.1%，低於預期，然管理層預期上下半年營收比重 45:55，預估在零組件價格穩
定後，訂單將穩定出貨。研究處預估 4Q26 營收 25.16 億元(+3.4%QoQ，
+8.6%YoY)，考量零組件漲價未能為轉轉嫁給客戶，毛利率 31.8%(+0.3pts QoQ，
-3.5pts YoY)，營利率 10%(+0.1pts QoQ，-2.7pts YoY)，稅後淨利 2.12 億元
(+41.6%QoQ，-27.5%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 1.92 元。2026 年受記憶體價格上漲，
客戶下單轉為保守，預估 2026 年營收 89.1 億元(+13.3%YoY)，毛利率 30.9%(-
4pts YoY)，營業淨利 6.29 億元(-1%YoY)，稅後淨利 4.15 億元(-10.6%YoY)，稅
後 EPS 為 3.76 元。 
美洲地區客戶將開花結果，2027 年獲利重返成長：展望 2027 年，若以地區區分，
虹堡北美及南美客戶進展順利，許多專案已獲得客戶認證將開始出貨；歐洲地區，
法國新銀行訂單將持續出貨；亞太地區，因日本基期已高，2027 年預估微幅成長。
整體來說， 2027 年營收及獲利將重回成長的一年，然需關注記憶體價格對獲利及
訂單影響。研究部預估 2027 年營收 90.5 億元(+1.6%YoY)，毛利率 31.5%(+0.6pts 
YoY)，營業淨利 8.21 億元(+30.6%YoY)，稅後淨利 5.56 億元(+33.9%YoY)，稅
後 EPS 為 5.04 元。

AI 高階測試需求維持強勁，成長趨勢不變 
◼ 產品組合有望優化，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元：CPC 及 VPC
稼動率皆維持高檔，預估 4Q26 營收 63.95 億元(+66.7%YoY)。獲利
方面，受惠產品組合持續優化，預估毛利率季增至 61.92%，隨營收
規模持續擴大，費用率有望年減至 25%，營益率 37.12%，稅後淨
利 20.75 億元(+119%YoY)，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元。 
◼ 產能擴充計畫持續推進，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 68.44 元：展望後
市，隨 AI HPC 需求持續成長，探針卡朝高頻高速、大電流及高針腳
數方向發展，帶動高階測試需求維持強勁。為解決產能瓶頸，旺矽
將積極擴充產能，2026/2027 年底 MEMS 月產能為 350 萬/600 萬
針，VPC 月產能為 200 萬/250 萬針，且 PCB 廠房預計 2H27 投產，
有助提升 PCB 自製率。整體而言，預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 
68.44/118.84 元，且 CPO 相關設備有望於 2028 年較明顯挹注營
收，預估 2028 年稅後 EPS 204.56 元。 
◼ 維持買進評等，目標價 6,500 元：評價方面，以 2028 年 EPS 估算，
目前 PE 約為 21X，低於旺矽近 2 年 30-55X，考量 AI 高階測試需求
持續強勁，且 CPO 布局有望挹注中長期成長動能，長線維持買進評
等，目標價調整為 6,500 元(2028F PE 32X)。

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓 (02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號         Page 2 of 6 
旺矽為全球前五大探針卡廠商  旺矽 1995 年 7 月於新竹成立，實收資本額 9.8 億元，截至 Apr-26，員工人數
共 2,110 人。主要業務包含晶圓探針卡測試及半導體設備銷售。旺矽提供一站
式購足服務，探針卡業務包含懸臂式探針卡(Cantilever Probe Cards)、垂直式
探針卡(Vertical Probe Cards)及微機電探針卡(MEMS)等；半導體設備則例如
光電自動化設備(PA)、溫控測試設備(Thermal)、先進半導體測試設備
(Advanced Semiconductor Test, AST)等。以 3Q26 產品別營收結構而言，探
針卡 72.7%，設備 25.4%，其他 1.9%。

3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.18 元，優於
預期

受惠 AI HPC 高階測試需求強勁，旺矽 3Q26 營收 59.04 億元(+12.8%QoQ，
+72.9%YoY)。獲利方面，由於產品組合優化，毛利率季增至 61.68%，且因費
用率較原先預期低，營業利益 22.76 億元(+26.3%QoQ，+154.1%YoY)。業外
認列匯兌利益後，稅後淨利 18.8 億元(+23.3%QoQ，+114.4%YoY)，3Q26 稅
後 EPS 19.18 元，優於原先預估之 18.07 元。

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓 (02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號         Page 3 of 6 
產品組合有望優化，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 21.18 元 
 由於 AI HPC 客 戶 拉 貨 動 能 持 續 強 勁 ， 旺 矽 Oct-26 營收 22.32 億元
(+8.98%MoM，+92.73%YoY)，優於預期。考量 CPC 及 VPC 稼動率皆維持高
檔，預估 4Q26 營收 63.95 億元(+8.3%QoQ，+66.7%YoY)。獲利方面，受惠
產品組合持續優化，預估毛利率季增至 61.92%，隨營收規模持續擴大，費用
率有望年減至 25%，營益率 37.12%，稅後淨利 20.75 億元(+10.4%QoQ，
+119%YoY)，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元。

產能擴充計畫持續推進，預 估
2026 年稅後 EPS 68.44 元

展望後市，隨 AI 與 HPC 需求持續成長，探針卡朝高頻高速、大電流及高針腳
數方向發展，帶動高階測試需求維持強勁。為解決產能瓶頸，旺矽將積極擴充
產能，包含：MEMS 月產能自 2025 年底 100 萬針，預期 2026 年底提升至 350
萬針，2027 年底目標為 600 萬針；VPC 月產能則自 2025 年底 120 萬針，預
期 2026 年底提升為 200 萬針，2027 年底有望再增加至 250 萬針；CPC 月產
能則維持持平略增。廠房方面，旺矽 Nov-26 中旬公告投入 22 億元進行湖口廠
機電工程，PCB 廠房預計如期於 2H27 投產，有助提升 PCB 自製率；另，
CPO 設備所需廠房改建，預計 1Q27 完成。資本支出方面，預估 2026/2027 年
分別為 15-18 億元及 20 億元，2027 年主要用於設備採購。

整體而言，投顧預估 2026 年營收 214.7 億元(+60.5%YoY)，受惠產品組合持
續優化，毛利率 60.5%，營益率 36%，稅後淨利 67.1 億元(+111.1%YoY)，上
修 2026 年稅後 EPS 至 68.44 元(原預估 64.97 元)。

Exhibit 6：旺矽擴產時程推估

Sources: Company，Taishin 
高階測試需求續強，預估 2027
年稅後 EPS 118.84 元 
 旺矽自 2026 年起整併 LED 與 AST 部門，並持續布局 CPO 檢測專案，目前主
要聚焦於 CPO insertion 2 之 EIC/PIC 測試及 insertion 3 之 die-level 測試應
用。由於 CPO 測試涉及光電整合，技術門檻及客製化程度較高，目前仍持續
與客戶進行驗證及開發。其中，insertion 2 既有客戶預計 2026 年底更新驗證
進度，另有新客戶持續接洽中，相關進展有望於 1H27 較為明朗；insertion 3
則已有多家國際客戶出貨工程機，且持續交機中，有望 1H27 逐步轉為量產機
需求。展望後市，隨 AI 高速傳輸需求提升，CPO 相關測試設備有望成為 AST
業務中長期成長動能，惟考量設備交期較長及客戶驗收認證所需時間，預期
2028 年營收貢獻將較為顯著，CPO 相關業務營收占比有望達雙位數。

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

TUẦN 02-06/11 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -16.26 điểm tương ứng -0.93%, với thanh 
khoản mở rộng

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Diễn biến phân hóa và biến động mạnh có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ vọng sẽ tiếp tục

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -16.26 điểm tương ứng -0.93%, với thanh khoản mở 
rộng. Thiết bị dụng cụ Y tế, Tiện ích khí và Bảo hiểm là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần qua. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị 
-870 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã MBB, VIC và VCB; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng 
ở mức 504 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng -2,010 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua 
và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -10,888 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 1,208 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -4,070 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam có tuần giảm điểm thứ 2 liên tiếp khi VN-Index lùi về kiểm định vùng hỗ trợ 
quanh 1.725 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm giảm hơn 30 điểm trước khi thu hẹp 
đà giảm nhờ lực cầu ở nhóm chứng khoán. Áp lực bán tập trung ở nhóm vốn hóa lớn, đặc 
biệt là bộ đôi Vingroup (VIC, VHM) cùng một số cổ phiếu ngân hàng và công nghệ, khiến 
chỉ số thiếu lực đỡ. Giao dịch của khối ngoại diễn biến trái chiều và giằng co — có phiên 
mua ròng trở lại ở một số mã, nhưng vẫn xen kẽ bán ròng ở nhóm trụ như VHM, VCB, cho 
thấy dòng vốn ngoại chưa xác lập xu hướng rõ ràng. Bối cảnh bên ngoài kém thuận lợi khi 
nhóm cổ phiếu công nghệ tại Mỹ bị bán tháo, kéo theo các chỉ số chính toàn cầu đồng 
loạt lao dốc. Mặc dù vậy, kỳ vọng vào động thái mở kênh hoán đổi ngoại tệ của NHNN để 
hỗ trợ thanh khoản, hạ lãi suất đã giúp tâm lý nhà đầu tư trong nước ổn định và không 
xuất hiện áp lực bán tháo.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến spinning thân nhỏ với bóng trên/dưới khá dài, 
cùng thanh khoản gia tăng, thể hiện sự giằng co mạnh nhưng về tổng thể lại cho thấy diễn 
biến đã cân bằng trở lại sau tuần giảm điểm trước đó. Trên khung ngày, mặc dù trải qua 
các phiên biến động tăng/giảm mạnh đan xen nhưng việc cho phản ứng bật tăng tích cực 
tại quanh đường biên dưới của mẫu hình tam giác đi ngang trung hạn cho thấy tương quan 
vẫn đang nghiêng về chiều hướng tăng. Diễn biến phân hóa và biến động mạnh có thể còn 
tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ tiếp tục hướng lên thử thách lại các vùng cản phía trên.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1800 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1700 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1660 (+-15)

### Page 4

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu 
Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích

trưởng 6%-8%/năm

chiếm 50% thị phần doanh thu ngành bán lẻ dược phẩm, từ mức 17%-20% hiện tại 
- Tiềm năng dài hạn từ mảng tiêm chủng tương đối tích cực

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years.1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.

risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,

## Document 316: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause, however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downside 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widely 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is 
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals”. By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutinised. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike. But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, the decision is justified by the need to further analyse risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that any rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United States). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the HICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1%-2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Anis Bensaidani   
Economist, United States, Japan

## Document 319: Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
09:49 November 13 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest
reading since late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of severe economic stress.
Renewed cost-of-living and interest rate pressures were front and centre following another lift in

inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage
holders are feeling the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group falling to a record low.

### Page 2

Consumers have become noticeably more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the relevant
sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge
up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business survey showed confidence slipped further
to –15 in October as conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic. Of concern for the
outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow materially faster than selling prices, further
compressing already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter financial conditions can jolt activity. The
value of new dwelling finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly decline in the series’
history, taking approvals 25% lower year-to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined with the housing tax changes announced in May.

Owner-occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home buyers proving particularly sensitive
to the deterioration in affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum across the domestic economy,
consistent with Westpac’s expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the

consensus view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the result, but core inflation was
also marginally stronger than forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the past four
months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost
inflation through. Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace, gaining 0.3% in
October.
The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor

Waller prior to the release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and the labour market while
showing significant concern over the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the passthrough
of energy and technology input inflation. His conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the
monthly rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward supporting an increase in our policy
rate of 25 basis points at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI inflation data out

later today are similar to the past two monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent or
even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also kept the focus on inflation risks and
stated or implied the FOMC needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s updates,
pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by
September. We believe this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core inflation ex

shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-month annualised basis and the labour market is
balanced; but it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate, US households are under pressure from
the cost of living, and their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing costs. Highlighting

### Page 3

this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and 2010. Our November Market Outlook will
provide a full update on our expectations for the US and the world when released on Westpac IQ this
afternoon.

英伟达⽀持的⼈⼯智能公司 Firmus 在搁置 IPO 计划后，正转向债务市场融资，以推进其在印度尼
西亚的数据中⼼项⽬。受 AI ⾏业估值承压及融资环境趋紧影响，该公司融资规模明显收缩，最新
债务融资⽬标较两个⽉前提出的⽅案⼤幅缩⽔。
11 ⽉ 23 ⽇，据彭博援引知情⼈⼠， Firmus 正考虑发⾏约 15 亿美元债券，作为⼀项总规模最⾼约
40 亿美元的综合债务融资⽅案的⼀部分。该⽅案还包括银⾏贷款和夹层债务，较公司两个⽉前
设定的约 75 亿美元融资⽬标减少近⼀半。Firmus 最快可能在今年年底前完成债券发⾏，但具体

时间仍取决于市场环境及其他条件。
融资所得将主要⽤于为 Firmus 与 DayOne Data Centers 合作开发的印尼数据中⼼采购英伟达最
新款 GB300 AI 芯⽚。在 AI 基础设施投资持续扩⼤的同时，市场对相关企业的估值和融资⻛险⽇
趋谨慎， Firmus 融资计划的调整也反映出这⼀变化。
IPO 受阻，转向债务融资
Firmus 上⽉搁置了 IPO 计划。此前，该公司原计划通过上市募资最⾼ 55 亿美元（含超额配售
权）。上市计划受阻后，公司转⽽寻求债务融资，以满⾜数据中⼼项⽬的资⾦需求。
报道援引知情⼈⼠称， Firmus 最近⼀周仍在与银⾏及投资者磋商融资⽅案。拟议中的债务融资
包括优先级债务和夹层债务，其中约 15 亿美元债券将与配套银⾏贷款共同构成优先级融资部
分。
债券和银⾏贷款的期限均为 69 个⽉，预计享有同等受偿顺序。债券的潜在收益率约为 8% ，银⾏
贷款利率则预计为 SOFR （有担保隔夜融资利率）加 325 个基点。
信⽤评级⽅⾯， Firmus 已从⾄少两家国际评级机构获得 BBB 投资级评级。摩根⼠丹利主导此次

融资， Firmus ⽬前正邀请其他银⾏加⼊承销团。
融资环境⽅⾯， AI 板块估值担忧持续发酵，全球借贷成本仍处⾼位，投资者对 AI 基础设施项⽬的
⻛险敞⼝更加审慎。 Firmus 回应媒体时表⽰： “Firmus 不对市场猜测发表评论，融资安排及任何
细节将在适当时候予以公告。 ”

## Document 363: Japan’s Economic Outlook for FY 2026–2028 (Nov. 2026)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Japan’s Economic Outlook for FY 2026–2028 (Nov. 2026).pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

< Real GDP growth forecast at 0.9% in FY 2026, 
1.3% in FY 2027, and 1.1% in FY 2028 > 
1. Real GDP growth in the July–September quarter of 2026 was −0.1% on an annualized basis, 
marking the first contraction in four quarters, mainly due to a decline in private consumption. 
2. Real GDP growth in the October–December quarter of 2026 and the January–March quarter of 
2027 is expected to remain in the low- to mid-0% range. This is due in part to weak private 
consumption, driven mainly by declining real purchasing power as inflation accelerates. 
Consequently, Japan’s economy is likely to remain sluggish in the second half of FY 2026. 
3. In FY 2027, lower inflation following the consumption tax rate cut on food and beverages is 
expected to improve households’ real purchasing power and lead to a recovery in private 
consumption. Meanwhile, growth in private non-residential investment is expected to accelerate as 
lower raw material prices improve corporate profits. 
4. Real GDP growth is forecast at 0.9% in FY 2026, 1.3% in FY 2027, and 1.1% in FY 2028. The 
consumption tax rate cut is estimated to lower real GDP by 0.1% in FY 2026 and raise it by 0.3% 
in FY 2027. 
5. Consumer price inflation (all items excluding fresh food) is expected to accelerate to the 3% range 
toward the end of FY 2026 as rising import prices feed through to a broad range of items, 
including food and daily necessities. However, it is projected to slow to the 1% range in FY 2027 
following the consumption tax rate cut on food and beverages. Overall, consumer price inflation 
(all items excluding fresh food) is forecast at 2.4% in FY 2026, 1.5% in FY 2027, and 2.0% in FY 
2028. 
NLI Research Institute 2026-11-24

Japan’s Economic Outlook for FY 2026–2028 
(Nov. 2026)

Taro Saito, Executive Research Fellow, Economic Research Department,  
tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

1 AI-related goods comprise the 103 product categories classified as “AI-Enabling-Products” by the WTO. AI-related goods accounted for 
17.1% of total exports in 2025.

### Page 3

AI-related goods have recorded pronounced
increases in both prices and volumes, likely
reflecting tighter supply and demand conditions
for semiconductors and electronic components as
global AI investment expands. Export prices of
AI-related goods rose sharply by 24.3% year-on-
year in the July–September quarter of 2026.
Together with the increase in export volumes,
this resulted in strong 38.0% year-on-year
growth in export value. Expanding AI-related

exports are substantially boosting total export value on both a volume and price basis.
These increases in export prices, led by AI-related goods, are also helping to mitigate the deterioration in the
terms of trade associated with heightened tensions in the Middle East. While higher prices for crude oil and
other commodities worsen the terms of trade through rising import prices, export prices have also increased
substantially. As a result, the deterioration in the terms of trade index—calculated by dividing export prices

by import prices—has been relatively limited.
Looking at past episodes of surging commodity prices, during the global commodity price surge around
2005–2008, import prices for crude oil and other commodities rose sharply while export price increases
failed to keep pace, leading to a deterioration in the terms of trade. From 2020 onward, the terms of trade
also deteriorated significantly due to rising commodity prices during the post-COVID-19 global economic

recovery, the surge in commodity prices following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine in 2022, and yen
depreciation. In both episodes, the cumulative deterioration in the terms of trade was at least 30%. By
comparison, the deterioration over the two quarters from the January–March quarter to the July–September
quarter of 2026 was 6.6%. A major difference from past episodes is that higher export prices, led by AI-
related goods, have partially offset the impact

of rising import prices caused by surging
crude oil prices.
However, import prices are still rising faster
than export prices, and the terms of trade
continue to deteriorate. If crude oil prices
remain elevated, income outflows abroad
could weigh on the domestic economy.
Meanwhile, if export prices continue to rise
as AI-related demand expands, this will help
mitigate further deterioration in the terms of
trade.

### Page 4

The wage hike rate in the 2027 Spring Labor Offensive (Shunto) is expected to slightly exceed
the previous year’s level
Real wages, calculated by deflating nominal wages using consumer prices (all items excluding imputed rent),
have maintained positive growth for nine consecutive months since January 2026. Nominal wage (total cash
earnings) growth accelerated from the low-2% range in 2025 to the 3% range after the start of 2026. In
addition, a substantial slowdown in inflation—partly due to government measures to ease the burden of

energy costs—has contributed to the increase in real wages.
Although real wages have recently begun to
recover, they had remained on a downward trend
for approximately four years from 2022, as
nominal wage growth failed to keep pace with
inflation. Compared with the pre-COVID-19
period in 2019, nominal wages in the July–
September quarter of 2026 were 12.6% higher,
whereas consumer prices (all items excluding
imputed rent) had risen by an even larger 16.8%

over the same period. Thus, despite the recovery
since the start of 2026, real wages remain 3.7%
below pre-COVID-19 levels, meaning nominal wage increases have not yet fully offset the erosion in
purchasing power caused by higher prices.
The environment surrounding the 2027 Spring Labor Offensive shows that labor market conditions remain
tight, with the active job openings-to-applicants ratio above 1 and the unemployment rate in the mid-2%
range. Corporate profits continue to reach new record highs despite the impact of heightened tensions in the

Middle East. Consumer price inflation had remained in the 1% range since the start of 2026 due to
government energy relief measures, but it rose to the 2% range in September for the first time in nine months
and is certain to accelerate.
In its basic framework for the 2027 Spring Labor Offensive announced on October 22, the Japanese Trade
Union Confederation (Rengo) set a wage hike guideline of “around 6%, with a minimum of 5% (including
the equivalent of regular pay increases),” raising its demands from the previous year’s “at least 5%.” It also

set a guideline of “¥20,000 and at least 6%” for unions at small and midsize companies, including an
additional amount to narrow wage disparities. In recent years, employers’ offers in the Spring Labor
Offensive have generally been slightly less than 1 percentage point below union demands. Since 2023, labor
unions’ wage demands have risen sharply due to high prices and labor shortages, and actual wage hike rates
have risen accordingly, keeping the gap between demands and offers relatively stable. Given this pattern, if

Rengo’s affiliated unions raise their wage demands in line with its policy, employers are also likely to raise
their offers, pushing up the overall wage hike rate.

### Page 5

Against this backdrop, the current outlook
forecasts a wage hike rate of 5.10% for the 2027
Spring Labor Offensive, remaining high in the
5% range for the fourth consecutive year and
rising slightly from 5.01% in the previous year
(on the Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare
basis, the forecast is 5.30% for 2027, compared
with the actual result of 5.18% in 2026). The
strong possibility that consumer price inflation
will accelerate from its current level of around

2% to the 3% range around the beginning of 2027—when wage negotiations get into full swing—should also
support wage increases.
Real wages have continued to grow by around 2% year-on-year, but growth is likely to slow gradually as
consumer price inflation accelerates, temporarily turning negative toward the end of FY 2026. However,
growth is expected to turn positive after the start of FY 2027 as the consumption tax rate cut on food and
beverages slows consumer price inflation. In FY 2028, once the effect of the consumption tax rate cut runs

its course, consumer price inflation will rise to around 2%. However, continued large wage hikes will sustain
nominal wage growth in the 3% range, keeping real wage growth at around 1%.
2. Real GDP growth forecast at 0.9% in FY 2026, 1.3% in FY 2027, and 1.1% in
FY 2028
Low growth expected to continue in the second half of FY 2026
The decline in private consumption—the main cause of negative growth in the July–September quarter of
2026—largely reflected the temporary effects of adverse weather. Even after these effects dissipate, however,

accelerating inflation is likely to hinder a recovery in consumption. Consumer price inflation returned to the
2% range in September 2026 for the first time in nine months, but is likely to rise to the 3% range toward the
end of FY 2026 as the pass-through of higher raw material prices to durable goods and daily necessities
becomes more widespread. Private consumption is therefore expected to remain weak in the second half of
FY 2026 as higher inflation erodes real purchasing power.

At present, growth is expected to turn positive in the October–December quarter of 2026, but with private
consumption struggling to grow, it is projected to reach only 0.6% on an annualized basis. In the January–
March quarter of 2027, private consumption is likely to decline quarter-on-quarter as inflation rises to the 3%
range and consumers hold off on purchases ahead of the consumption tax rate cut on food and beverages.
Real GDP growth is therefore likely to remain close to zero, at 0.2% on an annualized basis. Overall, Japan’s

economy is expected to remain sluggish in the second half of FY 2026, mainly because high prices depress
private consumption.

### Page 6

Private non-residential investment continues to recover on an underlying basis, supported by high corporate
profits, but the pace of recovery is likely to remain modest for the time being due to high construction
material prices and delays in construction progress caused by labor shortages. Although global AI-related
demand will support exports, overall growth is expected to remain modest given the persistent drag from the
situation in the Middle East and subdued shipments to China remaining amid weakness in its domestic

demand. As a result, exports are unlikely to drive economic growth.
In FY 2027, lower inflation following the consumption tax rate cut is expected to improve households’ real
purchasing power and lead to a recovery in private consumption. Meanwhile, growth in private non-
residential investment is expected to accelerate as lower raw material prices improve corporate profits.
Growth in the April–June quarter of 2027 is likely to be strong at 2.8% on an annualized basis, mainly driven

by a surge in private consumption as improved real purchasing power reinforces the rebound from pre-tax-
cut spending restraint. Thereafter, growth is expected to continue in the low-1% range on an annualized
basis—above the potential growth rate—led by domestic private demand such as private consumption and
non-residential investment. In FY 2028, although the effect of the consumption tax rate cut will fade, the
continued recovery in private consumption and ongoing increases in capital investment are likely to sustain

growth of around 1% on an annualized basis.
Real GDP growth is forecast at 0.9% in FY
2026, 1.3% in FY 2027, and 1.1% in FY 2028.
The institute estimates that consumers holding
off on purchases ahead of the consumption tax
rate cut on food and beverages will lower real
GDP in FY 2026 by 0.1%, while the subsequent
rebound and improved real purchasing power
from lower inflation will raise real GDP in FY
2027 by 0.3%.
Households’ real disposable income remains below its pre-COVID-19 level

Sluggish household consumption since the start of FY 2026 partly reflects the reclassification of spending as
government consumption following the introduction of free high school tuition and elementary school meals,
as well as adverse weather, including heavy and prolonged rain. Over a longer horizon, however, weak
growth in real disposable income is the primary cause. Households’ real disposable income (seasonally
adjusted) has recently recovered but remains slightly below its pre-COVID-19 level (the 2019 average).

### Page 8

Private consumption is forecast to grow by 0.0% in FY 2026, 1.4% in FY 2027, and 0.9% in FY 2028 year-
on-year.
Price outlook
The rate of increase in consumer prices (all items excluding fresh food, hereafter core CPI) fell to 1.9% year-
on-year in January 2026, dropping below 2% for the first time in three years and ten months (since March
2022). It subsequently remained in the 1% range before rising to 2.1% year-on-year in September, returning
to the 2% range for the first time in nine months.

The rate of increase in food prices (excluding fresh food) continued to slow after peaking at 8.3% year-on-
year in July 2025, reaching 2.7% in August 2026 before accelerating for the first time in 14 months to 2.9%
in September. According to Teikoku Databank’s “Survey of Price Trends at 195 Major Food Companies”
(released October 30), the number of food and beverage items subject to price increases in 2026 (January–
December) is 20,327, slightly below the 20,580 items recorded in the same period of the previous year. Amid

the surge in raw material prices associated with the deteriorating situation in the Middle East, the number of
items slated for price increases over the next three months has risen year-on-year since June 2026, although
the pace has slowed somewhat. The rate of increase in consumer food prices (excluding fresh food) is
expected to accelerate, though it should remain more moderate than in the summer of 2025, partly because
rice prices are expected to continue declining for the time being.

Meanwhile, energy prices have been restrained by the government’s relief measures, but support for
electricity and gas bills temporarily ended with September 2026 usage (reflected in October consumer
prices). Although support will resume for usage starting in December 2026, electricity and gas bills this
winter are expected to be substantially higher than in the previous year. This is because subsidy amounts will
be slightly lower than last year, and the impact of surging crude oil prices since the spring of 2026 will be

reflected in fuel cost adjustment rates with a lag. Additionally, to curb surging gasoline prices, the
government used subsidies to hold the nationwide average retail price of regular gasoline at approximately
¥170 per liter from March through November 2026, but it has announced plans to gradually reduce subsidies
from December onward and raise the benchmark price by ¥5 each month. Consequently, energy price
inflation is expected to accelerate sharply from −0.6% year-on-year in September 2026, reaching double

digits year-on-year after the start of 2027 and contributing more than 1 percentage point to core CPI
inflation.
This outlook assumes that support for electricity and gas bills will continue from the summer of 2027
onward, with subsidies gradually reduced. For gasoline subsidies, the benchmark price is assumed to be
raised in stages to ¥185 per liter in February 2027 and then held at that level.
Core CPI inflation is expected to continue trending upward during FY 2026 and reach the 3% range toward

the end of FY 2026 as rising import prices, led by crude oil, feed through to a broad range of items, including
energy, food, durable goods, and daily necessities. A mechanical estimate of the impact of the consumption
tax rate cut for food and beverages from April 2027 onward indicates that core CPI inflation will be reduced

## Document 381: OpenAI爆改旗舰GPU，AMD直接送厂流片！

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI爆改旗舰GPU，AMD直接送厂流片！.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

OpenAI 利⽤ GPT 重写 AMD 旗舰 GPU 电路代码，不到 5 个⽉实现最⾼ 14% 的 PPA 提升，部分
设计已送⼚流⽚。此前， GPT 已参与⾃研芯⽚设计并通过硅⽚验证。下⼀步， OpenAI 将联
合 Synopsys 训练新模型，让 AI 直接调⽤ EDA ⼯具，进⼀步提升芯⽚设计效率。
摘要：
AI 这把⽕，彻底烧进芯⽚圈了。
OpenAI 刚刚官宣，⾃家 GPT 给 AMD 旗舰 GPU 改电路，不到 5 个⽉，核⼼指标 PPA 最⾼提升
14% 。
要知道，这套设计 AMD ⼯程师，可是优化了好⼏年。
算下来， OpenAI ⼀上⼿直接把⼀年的进度条，暴⼒压缩到 5 个⽉。
AI 重写的电路，已经被 AMD 送去流⽚了。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

OpenAI 榨⼲芯⽚设计  5 个⽉再榨 14%
⼀块被顶级团队反复优化过的旗舰 GPU ，再往上挤⼀点都难。
⽤官博的原话说，这颗 AMD 旗舰芯⽚是「被⾼度优化过的设计」。
这⼀次， OpenAI 所做的，就是重写描述芯⽚电路的 SystemVerilog RTL 的代码。
从计算、控制、到数据搬运，所有核⼼电路，全在它的修改范围内。
⽽且，每改⼀处，它就调 Synopsys 的⼯具验⼀遍 ——
改完的模块⾏为跟原来⼀样， PPA （功耗、性能、⾯积）三头怎么取舍。

### Page 3

就这样，不到 5 个⽉的时间， AI 在 AMD 这套千锤百炼的旗舰 GPU IP 基线上，最⾼抠出了 14% 的
PPA 净提升！
OpenAI 是这么评价的， AI 在 RTL 层⾯的推理能⼒，已经达到了「专家级微架构师」的⽔平。
今天，总裁 Greg 在 DealBook 峰会上提到了这项成果，直⾔「我们已构建出更强⼤的 AI ，并取得
了巨⼤的经济价值」。
值得⼀提的是， AI 改出来的代码，已经在 AMD 内部⽤上了。
⼀部分放进最近送去制造的那版设计，另⼀部分写进⽣产设计⽂件，留给以后的版本。
就连 CPU 也有份， AMD ⾃⼰验过，⽤模型写的设计，下⼀代旗舰 CPU ⼏个关键模块的 PPA 提了
个位数百分⽐。
对此， AMD 掌⻔⼈苏妈亲⾃转发：「为这些了不起的成果骄傲」。

### Page 4

GPT-4o 魔改⾃研「辣椒」跑通验证了
这套打法， OpenAI 先在⾃家芯⽚  Jalapeño （辣椒）上⽤过。
⼀个乘法器，模型重新设计以后，综合出来的⾯积⼩了约 15% ，⼀次运算从 2 个时钟周期压到 1
个。
另⼀个加法器，⾯积也⼩了约 15% ，延迟还是 2 个周期。
整个算下来，向量单元的标准单元⾯积省了约 13% ，矩阵单元省了约 10% 。

### Page 5

这些不是仿真⾥的数，辣椒的硅⽚点亮时都验过了。
辣椒是 OpenAI 跟博通做的第⼀款⾃研推理芯⽚， 2025 年初团队就拿 GPT-4o 参与辅助设计。
新⼀代 GPT 越来越会造芯
不论是 AMD 旗舰 GPU ，还是辣椒，都是 OpenAI ⽤ AI 设计芯⽚的亮眼成绩单。
那么，「 GPT 全系家族」整体情况⼜如何呢？
OpenAI 专⻔搭了⼀套评测体系，挑了两款开源的 32 位 RISC-V 处理器核 ——PicoRV32 和 Ibex 。
主要考核两⼤任务。

### Page 6

先给到现成的 RTL 代码，让 GPT 优化现有设计，提升吞吐量。
其次，只给书⾯需求和接⼝模板，⾄于数据通路和流⽔线怎么切分，全由 AI ⾃⼰拍板。
最终，在每次约 11.2 万 token 的相近预算下，GPT-6.2 Astra 在两个内核上平均分都最⾼。
在优化 PicoRV32 这项上，它的得分约是 GPT-6 Astra 的两倍。
有意思的是，同等预算下，五款模型从头写的分数都⽐改现成的⾼。
毕竟，从 0 设计的⾃由度更⾼， GPT 顺⼿就把原核⾥⼀些⽤处不⼤的东西砍掉了，⽐如性能计数
器、额外指令
反正它们对跑分吞吐也没啥贡献，⾯积上限也放到了原设计的 16 倍。

## Document 387: Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Growth in advanced economies_ solid despite shocks (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 23 October 2026

EcoFlash

Q3 Growth in advanced economies: solid despite shocks

Growth looks to have held up in Q3 despite successive shocks (see our analysis), as it did in 2025 and H1 2026. A longer 
Middle East conflict has fed into more persistent inflation; drought has worsened in many countries, and interest rates 
have risen further – at the short end, with both the ECB and the Fed raising policy rates in September, and even more at 
the long end. However, our nowcasts show that growth is bending without breaking in most advanced economies. GDP 
figures (due on 29 October in the US and 30 October for the euro area and its member states) are expected to confirm 
that. In the Euro area, momentum looks stable (excluding Ireland, whose GDP is volatile). In Germany, growth has been 
uninterrupted since Q4 2025, while France is likely to have returned to growth after stagnating in H1. In the US, growth 
is likely to have gathered pace, according to the GDP Now, still driven by AI, but increasingly by other sectors as well.

Euro area: resilience beneath the surface. Our nowcast points to growth slowing to 0.3% in q/q, though the pace is stable 
excluding Ireland (Irish GDP is volatile and had markedly boosted the Q2 figure). Excluding Ireland, growth should hold at its H2 
average, a sign of the bloc’s underlying resilience. Also, momentum has broadened to a growing number of sectors (see our 
analysis). While industrial production stalled over the summer, services remained buoyant. The services PMI rebounded sharply 
in Q3 and sits firmly in expansion territory (53.0 in September). Improving job-related survey data (PMI and European Commission 
surveys) and a stable unemployment rate (6.4% in August) hint at more hiring in Q3 – a support for household consumption that 
partly offsets purchasing-power losses from persistent inflation. Corporate investment is underpinned by buoyant new investment 
lending (8.7% y/y In August, 12-month cumulative). 
France: growing, nevertheless. The succession of shocks does not appear to have prevented France from returning to 
growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q, according to our nowcast. One-off factors accounted for the stall in activity in H1. In Q3, the drought 
worsened and industrial production remained subdued. However, market services production rebounded in July and August, 
notably in business services, restaurants, digital and real estate. The latter points to residential investment recovering after a weak 
H1. Corporate investment likely followed, as reflected in buoyant credit flows and AI-related investment. Household consumption 
is likely to have improved slightly on higher electric vehicle sales.  
Euro area  France

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas  Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas

Germany: growth still solid. The H1 rebound in activity is moderating but continues. Our nowcast points to a fourth straight 
quarter of growth in Q3, at 0.2% q/q (after 0.3% in Q4 2025, 0.4% in Q1 2026, and 0.3% in Q2). Manufacturing activity eased 
slightly, according to the September PMI survey (53.9, down 0.4 m/m). Government spending will remain growth-supportive, with 
defence and infrastructure outlays gaining traction. Infrastructure spending continues to underpin activity in public works, which 
stands at its highest since November 2021 (2.5% 3m/3m in August). Exports (4.4% y/y between January and August) remain the 
main growth engine, helped by a pivot towards Europe (7.2%). 
United States: an expansion undeterred by inflation. Growth is likely to have gathered pace in Q3. The Atlanta Fed’s GDP 
Now points to a 3.5% annualized growth rate (AR), close to our own 3.7% forecast (0.9% non-AR) and up from 2.2% in 
Q2. Domestic demand remains the engine. Consumption is weathering the pickup in inflation, while the AI-driven capex cycle 
remains robust – as reflected in retail sales and nondefence capital-goods orders (excl. aircraft). As a result, growth in final sales

### Page 2

to private domestic purchasers1 should print above 4% AR again. By contrast, external trade should remain a drag on growth, 
reflecting AI-related import needs. Survey data point to momentum that is both solid and broad-based. The ISM indices have 
remained firmly in growth territory (54.9 quarterly average for manufacturing and services alike). At the same time, they highlight 
persistent price and supply-chain pressures.

Germany   United States

Source: Refinitiv, BNP Paribas   Source: Refinitiv, Atlanta Fed, BNP Paribas
